Below is an engineering logbook composed of numbered technical notes.
Section 1 (fabric-routing, revision 8): Direct attach copper cables link adjacent compute nodes in a physical ring topology without an intermediate switch. Each network interface maintains point-to-point subnet routes with jumbo frames enabled to reduce packet header overhead during sustained tensor transfers. In benchmark cycle 1, node ring telemetry observed 193 GB/s effective link utilization with median queue depth 4 and 95 percent cache hit efficiency across active workers.

Section 2 (memory-hierarchy, revision 15): Unified memory architectures share a single physical LPDDR5X pool between CPU cores and GPU streaming multiprocessors. Without swap space enabled, allocating beyond available physical pages triggers immediate process termination, so active daemons sample MemAvailable at short intervals. In benchmark cycle 2, node ring telemetry observed 206 GB/s effective link utilization with median queue depth 7 and 96 percent cache hit efficiency across active workers.

Section 3 (quantization-layout, revision 22): Low-bit ternary and mixed-precision tensor formats pack multiple quantized weights into compact byte streams alongside per-block floating-point scales. Attention projections, normalization layers, and routing gates remain in higher precision because small perturbations compound across residual streams. In benchmark cycle 3, node ring telemetry observed 219 GB/s effective link utilization with median queue depth 10 and 97 percent cache hit efficiency across active workers.

Section 4 (attention-caching, revision 29): Multi-head latent attention compresses key and value histories into a shared low-dimensional latent vector before caching. Sparse token indexers score candidate past positions so later decoder layers attend only to the highest-scoring subset of tokens. In benchmark cycle 4, node ring telemetry observed 187 GB/s effective link utilization with median queue depth 2 and 98 percent cache hit efficiency across active workers.

Section 5 (scheduler-queues, revision 36): Inference servers separate prompt prefill phases from autoregressive decode steps to avoid head-of-line blocking. During prefill, large matrix multiplications saturate compute units, whereas single-stream decode is bound primarily by memory bandwidth and inter-node synchronization latency. In benchmark cycle 5, node ring telemetry observed 200 GB/s effective link utilization with median queue depth 5 and 99 percent cache hit efficiency across active workers.

Section 6 (verification-logs, revision 43): Reproducible benchmarking records the exact binary commit hash, build flags, hardware topology, background processes, and wall-clock UTC timestamps alongside every throughput sample. Reporting individual warm runs and their spread exposes thermal throttling or host contention. In benchmark cycle 6, node ring telemetry observed 213 GB/s effective link utilization with median queue depth 8 and 95 percent cache hit efficiency across active workers.

Section 1376 (memory-hierarchy, revision 30): Unified memory architectures share a single physical LPDDR5X pool between CPU cores and GPU streaming multiprocessors. Without swap space enabled, allocating beyond available physical pages triggers immediate process termination, so active daemons sample MemAvailable at short intervals. Section 1377 (quantization-layout, revision 37): Low-bit ternary and mixed-precision tensor formats pack multiple quantized weights into compact byte streams alongside per-block floating-point scales. Attention projections, normalization layers, and routing gates remain in higher precision because small perturbations compound across residual streams. In benchmark cycle 1377, node ring telemetry observed 216 GB/s effective link utilization with median queue depth 7 and 96 percent cache hit efficiency across active workers.

Section 1378 (attention-caching, revision 44): Multi-head latent attention compresses key and value histories into a shared low-dimensional latent vector before caching. Sparse token indexers score candidate past positions so later decoder layers attend only to the highest-scoring subset of tokens. In benchmark cycle 1378, node ring telemetry observed 184 GB/s effective link utilization with median queue depth 10 and 97 percent cache hit efficiency across active workers.

Section 1379 (scheduler-queues, revision 51): Inference servers separate prompt prefill phases from autoregressive decode steps to avoid head-of-line blocking. During prefill, large matrix multiplications saturate compute units, whereas single-stream decode is bound primarily by memory bandwidth and inter-node synchronization latency. In benchmark cycle 1379, node ring telemetry observed 197 GB/s effective link utilization with median queue depth 2 and 98 percent cache hit efficiency across active workers.

Reporting individual warm runs and their spread exposes thermal throttling or host contention.
